# PREDICTION LEDGER — every claim, its grade date, and what it CHANGED

**Cory, 2026-08-18:** *"Still don't think we are making predictions, grading and
closing the loop. No one is in charge of it.."*

He is right about the ownership. Predictions were being made (preregs), some were
being graded, and **nothing connected the two or noticed when a grade never came.**
`DEFECT-REGISTER.md` has an enforced recheck date and
`draft/tools/register_recheck_check.js` fails the build on any open row past it.
Predictions had no equivalent. This is it.

## Who is in charge

**The relay owns this file.** Not "the lane that made the prediction" — that is how a
prediction goes quiet when its author moves on. The relay files the row, chases the
grade, and carries the consequence to whoever can act on it.

**`draft/tools/prediction_ledger_check.js` is what actually enforces it**, and it
fails the build on **two** things, not one:

1. a row past its **grade by** date that is still `OPEN` — the prediction nobody
   came back for;
2. a row marked `GRADED` whose **what changed** cell is empty — Cory, 2026-08-17:
   *"a grade that moved nothing"*. **A grade with no consequence is not a closed
   loop, it is a note.** `NOTHING — <reason>` is a legitimate consequence and passes;
   silence does not.

A row must carry an owner and a date. Rows with neither are themselves a defect.

**AND A NEW ROW MUST NAME ITS PRIOR-ART CHECK.** On 08-18 the relay filed four "new axis" hypotheses and **two of them had already been graded and failed** — pace (`pace_arm.json`, `clears: false`) and air-yards/EPA (`advanced_efficiency_study.json`, `clears: false`). Both were findable in seconds; neither was found, because nothing made looking cheap. **`python3 draft/tools/prior_art.py --grep <topic>` is now that check** — it prints every graded verdict in the repo (45 artifacts today) and deliberately does not judge relevance, because a filter that silently excluded the thing you needed is the failure it exists to prevent.

## Status vocabulary

`OPEN` = predicted, not yet graded · `GRADED` = measured, consequence recorded ·
`ABANDONED` = deliberately dropped, with the reason in **what changed**.

Run on the fresh 08-21 board. LEARNING TARGET: whether position-level predictability is a usable signal or a restatement of scoring level — decides if any arm may condition on it. SKILL DESIGN: de-mean and re-rank (raw `proj_sd` in place of `sd/mean`), Spearman against both the raw-sd order and the 1/mean order, plus a 4,000-shuffle permutation test on raw sd across the four skill positions; `Spearman(x,x)=+1.000` as the control. CONSEQUENCE ROUTE: SURVIVES → the ordering may be cited, magnitudes may not; COLLAPSES → the finding is retracted and any arm resting on it dies. | 08-21 | E | 08-21 | GRADED | **SURVIVES, and the magnitude does not.** Population matters and is stated: on starter-caliber players (QB12/RB30/WR36/TE12/K12/DEF12) the published ordering reproduces almost exactly — **K .203 · DEF .141 · TE .075 · RB .069 · WR .058 · QB .027** against the quoted K .193 · DEF .120 · TE .108 · RB .100 · WR .085 · QB .036. **De-meaned, the order holds: Spearman(sd/mean, raw sd) = +0.943** (only TE/RB swap, adjacent), and raw sd genuinely separates the skill positions — **permutation p = 0.0297** on a 5.06-pt observed spread. Control `Spearman(x,x) = +1.000`. **BUT: Spearman(sd/mean, 1/mean) = +0.886, and the spread collapses from 7.6× to 2.8× when the denominator is removed — 2.8× to 1.5× among the skill positions alone.** | **The ordering is citable; the ratio is not.** Anyone quoting *"K is 7.6× less predictable than QB"* is quoting a number that is mostly scoring level — on raw dispersion it is 2.8×, and QB-vs-the-other-skill-positions is ~1.5×. **⚠️ AND A POPULATION WARNING THAT IS THE REAL LESSON: measured over ALL 617 players carrying a `proj_sd`, the same statistic gives K .167 · DEF .130 · TE .333 · RB .475 · WR .334 · QB .393 — a COMPLETELY INVERTED ordering (RB > QB > WR > TE > K > DEF).** Deep bench players with near-zero projections make the denominator tiny and the ratio explode. I measured that first, saw the published claim apparently refuted 3-10×, and nearly filed it as a contradiction; matching the population is the only reason this row says SURVIVES instead. Rule 3i, on a statistic whose whole content is a ratio. RETIRES the de-mean question — the follow-up would be whether raw sd predicts realized weekly variance, which is P113's territory, not a new row of mine. |

Two candidate explanations remain undistinguished: (a) even a NEAR-PERFECT model built only from strictly-prior in-season history structurally cannot close this gap, because a meaningful share of the human edge comes from information no in-season-only reconstruction can ever carry (late-breaking inactive/injury news, beat-reporter role signals, weather) — in which case the backtest's own ceiling, not any projection formula, is the binding constraint; or (b) a genuinely different projection SOURCE (once real weekly external projections become retrievable in-season, starting 2026) closes most of the gap and this backtest has simply never had the right input available to test. **The bar: once 2026 in-season weekly data accumulates (own_weekly snapshots + real actuals, weekly_own_grade.py's ledger), re-run `lineup_edge_backtest`-style scoring using the ACTUAL captured own_weekly projection (not a reconstruction) for weeks with real history — if the edge vs. human still doesn't flip positive even with a real forward-looking projection in hand, (a) is confirmed; if it flips, (b) is confirmed and the backtest's historical ceiling was the artifact, not the tool.** Ungradable until enough 2026 weeks accumulate to run it (needs ≥4-6 graded weeks for a stable read) — filed now so the question doesn't go quiet the way P143's own predecessor concerns did. **⬆️ CONVERTED to `GRADING-POLICY.md`: (1) DECISION — which nine to start at lineup lock, per owner-week. (c) FALSE: carried forward, a player's own CV beats the band constant in 0 of 4 folds (−0.07, −0.26, +0.00, −0.04 MAE), and the comparison is GENEROUS to the player-CV arm because my constant is per-position while the shipped one uses finer position×band cells.** ⚠️ **THE CONTROL IS WHY ANY OF THIS IS READABLE: raw CV persistence on the same population reproduces the committed 0.52/0.39/0.47 at 0.50/0.39/0.47.** Not in doubt: the ceiling RATIO is hugely valuable — both arms cut MAE from ~7.4 to ~2.1 against level alone. It is the per-player refinement that earns nothing (register 61). | **THE ANSWER TO CORY'S "how can we measure upside better": FROM OUTCOMES, YOU CANNOT. IT ALSO REDIRECTS THE WHOLE QUESTION: every remaining hope for per-player upside is CROSS-SOURCE DISAGREEMENT — what forecasters disagree about, not what players did — which is exactly what the twelve-source ffanalytics ingest is for and why waiting for it is worth more than shipping the three-source mean early.** **→ P113** — the successor: does cross-source spread predict realised p90 better than the band constant, once the twelve sources land? Content is main's, unchanged.)* 🎯 **THE CLOSING-LINE BENCHMARK (the new captures' first learning target, filed BEFORE any 2026 line accrues): graded against Sunday closing lines — the sharpest public predictor that exists — our published weekly projection is CLOSER to realized outcomes than the Thursday-line-implied number at ≥2 of 4 positions over weeks 1-6.** LEARNING TARGET: whether our model adds anything to what the market already knows by Sunday — the honest ceiling on every projection claim we make, and the frame for WHERE to hunt edge (positions where we beat closing = real skill; where closing beats us = copy the market there). SKILL DESIGN: same players, same weeks, paired triple — our number vs Thursday-implied vs Sunday-closing-implied vs realized; closing-beats-opening is the store's own known-positive control (if closing is NOT better calibrated than Thursday, the capture is broken, not the market). CONSEQUENCE ROUTE: positions where we lose to closing → the weekly blend gains a closing-line arm THERE (market-following is a valid arm); positions where we beat closing → those are certified edge and the war-room/waiver tools weight our number harder. | 08-21 | D | 2026-10-20 | 🟡 OPEN | — | — |

Content is main's, unchanged.)* 🎯 **LINE MOVEMENT + BOOK DIVERGENCE AS SIGNALS (Bovada Thu→Sun pairs + DK/FD per-book quotes, filed before week 1): (a) Thursday→Sunday game-total movement predicts same-direction fantasy-scoring surprises for the teams involved; (b) when DK and FD disagree on a line by ≥1 point, the eventual consensus lands nearer the sharper book's side more often than chance.** LEARNING TARGET: whether movement/divergence earn places as weekly features — the SIAM pricing lesson operationalized (skill lives where pricing is wrong; two books disagreeing IS pricing being wrong somewhere). ## What P1–P4 cost, and what they bought

Four predictions, four grades, **four negatives**, all in one day, every one called
before the number existed. **Nothing shipped to the board from any of them** — which
is the point. The alternative history is a skill-weighted expert model and a
disagreement-based ceiling column, both live on Cory's board four days before a
draft, both delivering nothing, and neither one falsifiable after the fact because no
null was written down first.

**The three nulls did all the work:** the random-subset null killed P3, the shuffle
null killed P4, and the known-positive controls are what make "it found nothing"
mean something rather than "it cannot find anything."
